(i.e., informally, the time-reversed transition kernels) are fairly close to each other,
a crucial observation that suggests the validity of the diffusion generative model.

Furthermore, we are also in need of bounding the ratio of the two conditional distributions when going beyond the set
\mathcal{E}
.
As it turns out, it suffices to develop a crude bound on the logarithm of such ratios (which are used in defining the KL divergence), as stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 9
.

For all
(x_{t},x_{t-1})\in\real^{d}\times\real^{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\log\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}{p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}\leq 2T\left(\|x_{t-1}-\widehat{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+T^{2c_{R}}\right).

(83)

The proof of Lemma
9
is provided in Appendix
C.2
.

Step 3: bounding the KL divergence between
p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}
and
p_{Y^{\star}_{t-1}\,|\,Y_{t}}
.

Equipped with the above two lemmas, we are positioned to first control the KL divergence between
p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}
and
p_{Y^{\star}_{t-1}\,|\,Y_{t}}
;
by doing so we are neglecting the impact of the score estimation error for the moment (see the definition (
75
) of
Y^{\star}_{t-1}
).
It is first seen from Lemma
8
and (
76a
) that: for any
(x_{t},x_{t-1})\in\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}{p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(O\Big(d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\log^{2}T\Big)\Big)=1+O\bigg(d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\log^{2}T\bigg)

\displaystyle=1+O\bigg(d^{2}\frac{\log^{3}T}{T}\bigg)\in\Big[\frac{1}{2},2\Big],

(84)

where the last line results from (
39b
) and the assumption that
T\geq c_{10}d^{2}\log^{3}T
for some large enough constant
c_{10}>0
.
We can then calculate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\mathcal{E}}+\int_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}}\Big)p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\log\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}{p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\Bigg\{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})-p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\cdot O\Bigg(\bigg(\frac{p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}-1\bigg)^{2}\Bigg)\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\log\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}{p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\Bigg\{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})-p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})+p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})O\bigg(d^{4}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{4}T\bigg)\Bigg\}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\Big\{2T\big(\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|x_{t-1}-\widehat{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+T^{2c_{R}}\big)\Big\}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}.

(85)

Here,
(\text{i})
results from the elementary fact that: if
\big|\frac{p_{Y}(x)}{p_{X}(x)}-1\big|<\frac{1}{2}
, then the Taylor expansion gives

\displaystyle p_{X}(x)\log\frac{p_{X}(x)}{p_{Y}(x)}

\displaystyle=-p_{X}(x)\log\Big(1+\frac{p_{Y}(x)-p_{X}(x)}{p_{X}(x)}\Big)

\displaystyle=p_{X}(x)-p_{Y}(x)+p_{X}(x)O\bigg(\Big(\frac{p_{Y}(x)}{p_{X}(x)}-1\Big)^{2}\bigg);

regarding (ii), we invoke (
84
) and Lemma
9
.

To continue, let us bound each term on the right-hand side of (
85
) separately.
From the definition of the set
\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
80
)), direct calculations yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((X_{t},X_{t-1})\notin\mathcal{E}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{(x_{t},x_{t-1})\notin\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t-1}}(x_{t-1})p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{t-1}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{t-1})\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{(x_{t},x_{t-1})\notin\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t-1}}(x_{t-1})\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\alpha_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-c_{3}d\log T\big),

(86)

and similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{(x_{t-1},x_{t})\notin\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\Big(2T\big(\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|x_{t-1}-\widehat{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+T^{2c_{R}}\big)\Big)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-c_{3}d\log T\big).

(87)

In addition, for every
(x_{t},x_{t-1})
obeying
\|x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\|_{2}>c_{3}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}
and
-\log p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{6}d\log T
,
one can use the definition (
74b
) of
\mu_{t}^{\star}(\cdot)
to obtain

\displaystyle\|x_{t-1}-\mu_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|x_{t-1}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\mathbb{E}\Big[x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\mid X_{t}=x_{t}\Big]\bigg\|_{2}

(88)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg\|x_{t-1}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}\bigg\|_{2}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big\|_{2}\mid X_{t}=x_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq c_{3}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}-6\overline{c}_{5}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}}\sqrt{d\log T}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(c_{3}-6\overline{c}_{5}\frac{\sqrt{1-\alpha_{t}}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}}\Bigg)\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}\geq\frac{c_{3}}{2}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T},

(89)

where the third line results from (
43a
) in Lemma
1
,
and the last line applies (
39
)
and holds true as long as
c_{3}
is large enough.
In turn, this combined with (
76a
) indicates that: for any
x_{t}
obeying
-\log p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{6}d\log T
,

\displaystyle\int_{x_{t-1}:\|x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\|_{2}>c_{3}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}}p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{c_{3}}{2}d\log T\Big).

(90)

As a result, (
86
) and (
90
) taken collectively demonstrate that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\Big\{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})-p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\Big\}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\left|1-\mathbb{P}\big((X_{t},X_{t-1})\notin\mathcal{E}\big)-\int_{(x_{t},x_{t-1})\notin\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\Big\{1-p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(x_{t-1}\,|\,x_{t})\Big\}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{c_{3}}{2}d\log T\Big).

(91)

Substituting (
87
) and (
91
) into (
85
) yields: for each
t\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]\lesssim d^{4}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{4}T+3\exp\Big(-\frac{c_{3}}{2}d\log T\Big)\lesssim\frac{d^{4}\log^{6}T}{T^{2}},

(92)

where the last inequality utilizes the properties (
39
) of the learning rates.

Step 4: quantifying the effect of score estimation errors.

Thus far, we have quantified the KL divergence between
p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}
and
p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}
.
Given that
Y_{t-1}^{\star}
is obtained from
Y_{t}
using the true score function,
we still need to control the effect of the score estimation error,
which is accomplished by means of the following lemma. The proof of this lemma can be found in Appendix
C.3
.

Lemma 10
.

For any
1<t\leq T
, one has

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\exp\big(-c_{20}d\log T\big)+\frac{d\log^{3}T}{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}\sim q_{t}}\big[\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(X_{t})^{2}\big]

(93)

for some universal constant
c_{20}>0
.

Step 5: putting all this together.

To finish up, substitute (
92
), (
179
) and (
179
) into the decomposition (
79
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_{1}}\parallel p_{Y_{1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_{T}}\parallel p_{Y_{T}})+\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left\{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}\,|\,Y_{t}}(\cdot\,|\,x_{t})\Big)\Big]\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_{T}}\parallel p_{Y_{T}})+\sum_{2\leq t\leq T}\frac{d^{4}\log^{6}T}{T^{2}}+\left(d\log^{3}T\right)\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(X_{t})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{d^{4}\log^{6}T}{T}+d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score}}^{2}\log^{3}T,

where the last relation applies the bound on
\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_{T}}\parallel p_{Y_{T}})
as in (
122
).
This establishes Theorem
3
.

6
Discussion

In this paper, we have developed a new suite of non-asymptotic theory for establishing the convergence and faithfulness of diffusion generative modeling,
assuming access to reliable estimates of the (Stein) score functions.
Our analysis framework seeks to track the dynamics of the reverse process directly using elementary tools,
which eliminates the need to look at the continuous-time limit and invoke the SDE and ODE toolboxes.
Only very minimal assumptions on the target data distribution are imposed.
In addition to demonstrating the non-asymptotic iteration complexities of two mainstream discrete-time samplers — a deterministic sampler based on the probability flow ODE, and a DDPM-type stochastic sampler — we have discovered potential strategies to further accelerate the sampling processes, taking advantage of estimates of a small number of additional objects.
The analysis framework laid out in the current paper might shed light on how to analyze other variants of score-based generative models as well.

Moving forward, there are plenty of questions that require in-depth theoretical understanding.
For instance, the dimension dependency in our convergence results remains sub-optimal; can we further refine our theory in order to reveal tight dependency in this regard?
Can we establish sharp convergence results in terms of the Wasserstein distance, which
could sometimes be “closer” to how humans differentiate pictures and might potentially help relax Assumption
2
in the case of deterministic samplers?
To what extent can we further accelerate the sampling process, without requiring much more information than the score functions?
Ideally, one would hope to achieve accleration with the aid of the score functions only.
It would also be of paramount interest to establish end-to-end performance guarantees that take into account both the score learning phase and the sampling phase.

Appendix A
Proof for several preliminary facts

A.1
Proof of properties (
38
)

Elementary calculations reveal that: the
(i,j)
-th entry of
J_{t}(x)
is given by

\displaystyle\big[J_{t}(x)\big]_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\ind\{i=j\}+\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg\{\Big(\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x_{i}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0,i}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\Big)\Big(\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x_{j}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0,j}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x_{i}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0,i}\big)\big(x_{j}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0,j}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\bigg\}.

(94)

This immediately establishes the matrix expression (
38
).

A.2
Proof of properties (
39
) regarding the learning rates

Proof of property (
39a
).

From the choice of
\beta_{t}
in (
22
), we have

\alpha_{t}=1-\beta_{t}\geq 1-\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\geq\frac{1}{2},\qquad t\geq 2.

The case with
t=1
holds trivially since
\beta_{1}=1/T^{c_{0}}
for some large enough constant
c_{0}>0
.

Proof of properties (
39b
) and (
39c
).

We start by proving (
39b
).
Let
\tau
be an integer obeying

\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{\tau}\leq 1<\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{\tau+1},

(95)

and we divide into two cases based on
\tau
.

•

Consider any
t
satisfying
t\leq\tau
.
In this case, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle 1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{t}.

(96)

Clearly, if (
96
) is valid, then any
t\leq\tau
obeys

\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\frac{\beta_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t}}{\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t}}=\frac{3c_{1}\log T}{T}

as claimed. Towards proving (
96
),
first note that the base case with
t=2
holds true trivially since
1-\overline{\alpha}_{1}=1-\alpha_{1}=\beta_{1}\geq\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{2}/3
.
Next, let
t_{0}>2
be
the first time
that Condition (
96
) fails to hold and suppose that
t_{0}\leq\tau
.
It then follows that

\displaystyle 1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}=1-\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}}{\alpha_{t_{0}-1}}\leq 1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}<\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{t_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{t_{0}-1}<\frac{1}{2},

(97)

where the last inequality result from (
95
) and the assumption
t_{0}\leq\tau
.
This taken together with the assumptions (
96
) and
t_{0}\leq\tau
implies that

\frac{(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\geq\frac{\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\beta_{1}\min\Big\{\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t_{0}-1},1\Big\}\cdot\big(1-\frac{1}{2}\big)}{\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t_{0}-1}}=\frac{\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t_{0}-1}}{\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{t_{0}-1}}=\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}.

As a result, we can further derive

\displaystyle 1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-1}

\displaystyle=1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}=1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}+(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(1+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t_{0}-1})\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2}}\bigg)(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2})

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t_{0}-2})\geq\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)\cdot\bigg\{\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{t_{0}-1}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{t_{0}},

where the penultimate line holds since (
96
) is first violated at
t=t_{0}
;
this, however, contradicts with the definition of
t_{0}
.
Consequently, one must have
t_{0}>\tau
, meaning that (
96
) holds for all
t\leq\tau
.

•

We then turn attention to those
t
obeying
t>\tau
.
In this case, it suffices to make the observation that

1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}\geq 1-\overline{\alpha}_{\tau-1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\bigg)^{\tau}=\frac{\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\big)^{\tau+1}}{1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}}\geq\frac{1}{4},

(98)

where the second and the third inequalities come from (
96
).
Therefore, one obtains

\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}}{1/4}\leq\frac{4c_{1}\log T}{T}.

The above arguments taken together establish property (
39b
).

In addition, it comes immediately from (
39b
) that

1\leq\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=1+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=1+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\leq 1+\frac{4c_{1}\log T}{T},

thereby justifying property (
39c
).

Proof of property (
39d
).

Turning attention to the second claim (
39d
),
we note that for any
t
obeying
t\geq\frac{T}{2}\gtrsim\frac{T}{\log T}
,
one has

1-\alpha_{t}=\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\min\bigg\{\beta_{1}\Big(1+\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\Big)^{t},\,1\bigg\}=\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}.

This in turn allows one to deduce that

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{T}\leq\prod_{t:t\geq T/2}\alpha_{t}\leq\Big(1-\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T}\Big)^{T/2}\leq\frac{1}{T^{c_{2}}}

for an arbitrarily large constant
c_{2}>0
.

Proof of property (
39e
).

Finally, it is easily seen from the Taylor expansion that the learning rates
\{\alpha_{t}\}
satisfy

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{d/2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(1+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)^{d/2}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{d(d-2)(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{8(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}+O\bigg(d^{3}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{3}\bigg),

provided that
\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim 1
.

A.3
Proof of Lemma
1

To establish this lemma, we first make the following claim, whose proof is deferred to the end of this subsection.

Claim 1
.

Consider any
c_{5}\geq 2
and suppose that
c_{6}\geq 2c_{R}
. There exists some
x_{0}\in\real^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}-y\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\qquad\quad\text{and}

(99a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\|X_{0}-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon\big)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{T^{2\theta(y)}}\Big)^{d}\qquad\quad\text{with}\quad\epsilon=\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}/2}}

(99b)

hold simultaneously, where
c_{0}
is defined in (
22
).

With the above claim in place, we are ready to prove Lemma
1
.
For notational simplicity, we let
X
represent a random vector whose distribution
p_{X}(\cdot)
obeys

\displaystyle p_{X}(x)=p_{X_{0}\mid X_{t}}(x\,|\,y).

(100)

Consider the point
x_{0}
in Claim
1
, and let us look at a set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\Big\{x:\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}>4c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\Big\},

where
c_{5}\geq 2
(see Claim
1
).
Combining this with property (
99a
) about
x_{0}
results in

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X-y\|_{2}>5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(X\in\mathcal{E}).

(101)

Consequently, everything boils down to bounding
\mathbb{P}(X\in\mathcal{E})
.
Towards this, we first invoke the Bayes rule
p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x\,|\,y)\propto p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)
to derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{E}\,|\,X_{t}=y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{x\in\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{x}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\int_{x\in\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{x:\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sup_{x\in\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)}{\inf_{x:\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)}\cdot\frac{\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{E})}{\mathbb{P}(\|X_{0}-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon)}.

(102)

To further bound this quantity,
note that: in view of the definition of
\mathcal{E}
and
expression (
99a
), one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{E}}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{x:\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\|_{2}>4c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x:\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}>3c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{9c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T}{2}\bigg)

and

\displaystyle\inf_{x:\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\inf_{x:\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|y-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\inf_{x:\|x-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|y-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}-\frac{\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|y-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T-\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}}}\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\big(-2c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T\big),

where the second line is due to the elementary inequality
\|a+b\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|a\|_{2}^{2}+2\|b\|_{2}^{2}
,
the penultimate line relies on (
99
),
and the last line holds true since
1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}\geq 1-\alpha_{1}=1/T^{c_{0}}
(see (
22
)).
Substitution of the above two displays into (
102
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{E}\,|\,X_{t}=y)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-2.5c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T\big)\cdot\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}(\|X_{0}-x_{0}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-2.5c_{5}^{2}\theta d\log T\big)\cdot\left(T^{2\theta(y)+c_{0}/2}\right)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-(2.5c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)-2\theta(y)-c_{0}/2)d\log T\big),

(103)

where the second inequality invokes (
99b
).
Substituting this into (
101
) and recalling the distribution (
100
) of
X
,
we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X-y\|_{2}>5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-(2.5c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)-2\theta(y)-c_{0}/2)d\log T\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T\big),

with the proviso that
c_{5}\geq 2
and
c_{6}\geq c_{0}
(so that
\theta(y)\geq c_{6}\geq c_{0}
).
This concludes the proof of the advertised result (
42
) when
c_{5}\geq 2
and
c_{6}\geq 2c_{R}+c_{0}
,
as long as Claim
1
can be justified.

With the above result in place, it then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big\|_{2}\,\big|\,X_{t}=x_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big\|_{2}\ind\big\{\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\|_{2}\geq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\big\}\,\Big|\,X_{t}=x_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\int_{5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\|_{2}\geq\tau\,|\,X_{t}=x_{t}\big)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\int_{5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}^{\infty}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\tau^{2}}{25(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\exp\big(-c_{5}^{2}\theta(y)d\log T\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 6c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T},

as claimed in (
43a
) by taking
c_{5}=2
.
The proofs for (
43b
), (
43c
) and (
43d
) follow from similar aguments and are hence omitted for the sake of brevity.

Proof of Claim
1
.

We prove this claim by contradiction.
Specifically, suppose instead that:
for every
x
obeying
\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}\leq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T},
we have

\mathbb{P}(\|X_{0}-x\|_{2}\leq\epsilon)\leq\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2T^{\theta(y)}R}\bigg)^{d}\qquad\text{with }\epsilon=\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}/2}}.

(104)

Clearly, the choice of
\epsilon
ensures that
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}
.
In the following, we would like to show that this assumption leads to contradiction.

First of all, let us look at
p_{X_{t}}
, which obeys

\displaystyle p_{X_{t}}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{x}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{x:\,\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}\geq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{x:\,\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}<c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x.

(105)

To further control (
105
),
we make two observations:

1)

The first term on the right-hand side of (
105
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{x:\,\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}\geq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{z:\,\|z\|_{2}\geq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad<\frac{1}{2}\exp\big(-\theta(y)d\log T\big),

(106)

provided that
c_{5}\geq 2
and
c_{6}>0
is large enough (note that
\theta(y)\geq c_{6}
).
Here, we have used
X_{t}\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}W
with
W\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})

as well as standard properties about Gaussian distributions.

2)

Regarding the second term on the right-hand side of (
105
),
let us construct an epsilon-net

\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}=\{z_{i}\}
for the following set

\big\{x:\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}\leq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\text{ and }\|x\|_{2}\leq R\big\},

so that for each
x
in this set, one can find a vector
z_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|x-z_{i}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
.
Clearly, we can choose
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
so that its cardinality obeys
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq(2R/\epsilon)^{d}
.
Define
\mathcal{B}_{i}\coloneqq\{x\mid\|x-z_{i}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon\}
for each
z_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
.
Armed with these sets, we can derive

\displaystyle\int_{x:\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}<c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{0}}(x)p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(y\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{-d/2}\sum_{i=1}^{|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|}\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{B}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{-d/2}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2T^{2\theta(y)}R}\bigg)^{d}\bigg(\frac{2R}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{d}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\exp\big(-\theta(y)d\log T\big),

where the penultimate step comes from the assumption (
104
).

The above results taken collectively lead to

\displaystyle p_{X_{t}}(y)

\displaystyle<\exp\big(-\theta(y)d\log T\big),

(107)

thus contradicting the definition (
41
) of
\theta(y)
.

Consequently, we have proven the existence of
x
obeying

\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x-y\|_{2}\leq c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}
and

\mathbb{P}(\|X_{0}-x\|_{2}\leq\epsilon)>\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2T^{\theta(y)}R}\bigg)^{d}\geq\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{T^{2\theta(y)}}\bigg)^{d},

provided that
\theta(y)\geq c_{6}\geq 2c_{R}
.
This completes the proof of Claim
1
.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
2

For notational convenience, let us denote

\widehat{x}_{t}=x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}
throughout the proof.
As a key step of the proof, we note that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle p_{X_{t-1}}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})\cdot\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t})\cdot\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\mathrm{d}x_{0},

(108)

thus establishing a link between
p_{X_{t-1}}(x)
and
p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})
.
Consequently, in order to control
p_{X_{t-1}}(x)
,
it is helpful to first look at the density ratio
\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}
,
which we accomplish in the sequel.

Recall that
X_{t}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}}\,X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}}\,W
with
W\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.
In what follows, let us consider any
x_{t},x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
x
obeying

\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\|_{2}\leq c_{3}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}

(109)

for some constant
c_{3}>0
.
The density ratio of interest satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}=\bigg(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\bigg)^{d/2}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\exp\Bigg(\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})+\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}\Bigg),

(110)

where the last inequality follows from the two relations below:

\log\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\log\left(1+\frac{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\right)\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}-2\big\langle\widehat{x}_{t}-x,\widehat{x}_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\rangle}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2\alpha_{t}(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}.

Next, let us define the following set given
x_{t}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Big\{x_{0}:\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}\leq c_{4}\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\Big\}

(111)

for some large enough numerical constant
c_{4}>0
, and we shall look at
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{c}}
separately.
Towards this, we make the following observations:

•

For any
x_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
, one can utilize (
111
) and (
109
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})+\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T+d\sqrt{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\alpha_{t})}\log T}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(d\log T)\left\{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}+\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T,

(112)

where the last inequality makes use of the facts
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\leq 1
(cf. (
39b
)) and

\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\alpha_{t}\leq 2.

(113)

Moreover, the properties (
39
) of the stepsizes tell us that

d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T\lesssim d\sqrt{\frac{\log^{3}T}{T}}\leq c_{10}

for some small enough constant
c_{10}>0
, as long as
T\geq c_{11}d^{2}\log^{3}T
for some sufficiently large constant
c_{11}>0
.
Taking this together with (
110
) and (
112
) reveals that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}

\displaystyle=1+O\bigg(d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T\bigg),

(114)

with the proviso that (
109
) holds and
x_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
.

•

Instead, if
x_{0}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
, then one can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})+\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})+\big(1+\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\big)\big\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\big\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})+c_{3}\big(1+\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\big)d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}+\frac{2(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\frac{c_{3}d\big(1-\alpha_{t}+1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}\big)\log T}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\cdot\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{c_{4}d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})\log T}+\frac{2(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2c_{3}}{c_{4}}\cdot\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}+\frac{8c_{1}\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\log T}{T(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1})}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}\left(\frac{2c_{3}}{c_{4}}+\frac{8c_{1}\log T}{T}\right)\frac{2\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\leq\frac{8c_{3}}{c_{4}}\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}},

where (i) comes from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (ii) is valid
due to the assumption on
\|\widehat{x}_{t}-x\|_{2}
, (iii) follows
from the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
, and (iv) is a consequence
of (
113
). Substitution into (
110
) leads to

\displaystyle\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\leq\exp\Bigg(\frac{8c_{3}}{c_{4}}\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg),

(115)

provided that (
109
) holds and
x_{0}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
.

In light of the above calculations, we can invoke (
116
) to demonstrate that: for any
x
obeying (
109
),

\displaystyle p_{X_{t-1}}(x)

\displaystyle=p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}+\int_{x_{0}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}\right)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t})\cdot\frac{p_{X_{t-1}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x_{t}\,|\,x_{0})}\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\int_{x_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}\Bigg(1+O\bigg(d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T\bigg)\Bigg)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad+p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\int_{x_{0}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}O\left(\exp\Bigg(\frac{8c_{3}}{c_{4}}\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg)\right)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{0}.

(116)

By virtue of Lemma
1
, if
-\log p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{6}d\log T
for some large constant
c_{6}>0
,
then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\big\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x_{t}\big\|_{2}\geq 5c_{5}\sqrt{d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\mid X_{t}=x_{t}\right\}\leq\exp(-c_{5}^{2}c_{6}d\log T)

(117)

for any
c_{5}\geq 2
.
Some elementary calculation then reveals that

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}\exp\Bigg(\frac{8c_{3}}{c_{4}}\frac{\big\|x_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Bigg)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}}}

(118)

with
c_{0}
defined in (
22
),
provided that
c_{4}/c_{3}
is sufficiently large.
Hence, substituting it into (
116
) demonstrates that, for any
x
satisfying (
109
),

\displaystyle p_{X_{t-1}}(x)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(1+O\bigg(d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T\bigg)\Bigg)\big(1-o(1)\big)p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})+O\bigg(\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}}}\bigg)p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})

(119)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(1+O\bigg(d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T+\frac{1}{T^{c_{0}}}\bigg)\Bigg)p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\Bigg(1+O\bigg(d\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\log T\bigg)\Bigg)p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})

(120)

\displaystyle\in\left[\frac{1}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t}),\,\frac{3}{2}p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\right],

(121)

where the penultimate inequality holds since (according to (
22
))

\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\geq\sqrt{1-\alpha_{t}}=\sqrt{\beta_{t}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{c_{1}\log T}{T^{c_{0}+1}}},

and the last inequality holds due to (
39b
) and the condition that
T/(d^{2}\log^{3}T)
is sufficiently large.
In other words, (
121
) reveals that
p_{X_{t-1}}(x)
is sufficiently close to
p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})
.

We are now ready to establish our claim. In view of the assumption (
44
), we have

\big\|x_{t}(\gamma)-\widehat{x}_{t}\big\|_{2}=\big\|\gamma x_{t-1}+(1-\gamma)\widehat{x}_{t}-\widehat{x}_{t}\big\|_{2}=\gamma\big\|x_{t-1}-\widehat{x}_{t}\big\|_{2}\leq c_{3}\sqrt{d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}.

Therefore, taking
x
to be
x_{t}(\gamma)
in (
121
) tells us that: if
-\log p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{6}d\log T
, then

-\log p_{X_{t-1}}\big(x_{t}(\gamma)\big)\leq-\log p_{X_{t}}(x_{t})+\log 2\leq c_{6}d\log T

as claimed.

A.5
Proof of Lemma
3

Recognizing that
Y_{T}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
and that

X_{T}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T}}\,\overline{W}_{t}
with
\overline{W}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
(independent from
X_{0}
),
one has

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_{T}}\parallel p_{Y_{T}})

\displaystyle=\int p_{X_{T}}(x)\log\frac{p_{X_{T}}(x)}{p_{Y_{T}}(x)}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\int p_{X_{T}}(x)\log\frac{\int_{y:\|y\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}p_{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}X_{0}}(y)p_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T}}\,\overline{W}_{t}}(x-y)\mathrm{d}y}{p_{Y_{T}}(x)}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int p_{X_{T}}(x)\log\frac{\sup_{y:\|y\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}p_{\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T}}\,\overline{W}_{t}}(x-y)}{p_{Y_{T}}(x)}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int p_{X_{T}}(x)\Bigg(-d/2\log(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})+\sup_{y:\|y\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}\bigg(-\frac{\|x-y\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})}+\frac{\|x\|_{2}^{2}}{2}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{\leq}}\int p_{X_{T}}(x)\bigg(-d/2\log(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})+\|x\|_{2}\sup_{y:\|y\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}\frac{\|y\|_{2}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{T}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-d/2\log(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})+\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})}\mathbb{E}\left[\|X_{T}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{iii})}}{{\lesssim}}\overline{\alpha}_{T}d+\frac{\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{T})}\left(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}+\sqrt{d}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{iv})}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{T^{200}},

(122)

where
(\text{i})
arises from the assumption that
\|X_{0}\|_{2}\leq T^{c_{R}}
,
(ii) applies the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (iii) holds true since

\mathbb{E}\left[\|X_{T}\|_{2}\right]\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}\|X_{0}\|_{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\overline{W}_{t}\|_{2}\right]\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\overline{W}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]}\leq\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}}T^{c_{R}}+\sqrt{d},

and (iv) makes use of (
39d
) given that
c_{2}\geq 1000
.
The proof is thus completed by invoking the Pinsker inequality
(
Tsybakov, 2009
, Lemma 2.5)
.

Appendix B
Proof of auxiliary lemmas for the ODE-based sampler

B.1
Proof of Lemma
4

B.1.1
Proof of relations (
57
) and (
58a
)

Recall the definition of
\phi_{t}
and
\phi_{t}^{\star}
in (
51
),
and introduce the following vector:

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\coloneqq x-\phi_{t}(x)=x-\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)+\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)-\phi_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\int_{x_{0}}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x_{0}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2}\big(s_{t}(x)-s_{t}^{\star}(x)\big).

(123)

The proof is composed of the following steps.

Step 1: decomposing
p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)/p_{X_{t}}(x)
.

Recognizing that

X_{t}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,W\qquad\text{with }W\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})

(124)

and making use of the Bayes rule, we can express the conditional distribution
p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)
as

\displaystyle p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)=\frac{p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}p_{X_{t}\,|\,X_{0}}(x\,|\,x_{0})=\frac{p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\cdot\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg).

(125)

Moreover, it follows from (
124
) that

\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\big(\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t-1}}\,W\big)=\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}+\sqrt{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\,W.

(126)

These taken together allow one to rewrite
p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}
such that:

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{=}\frac{1}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\big\|\phi_{t}(x)-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{1}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})\frac{1}{\big(2\pi(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\big)^{d/2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{=}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{d/2}\cdot\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{=}\left\{1+\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+O\bigg(d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\bigg)\right\}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}.

(127)

Here, identity (i) holds due to (
126
)
and hence

p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}(x)=\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}}(x_{0})p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}W}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0};

identity (ii) follows from (
123
) and elementary algebra;
relation (iii) is a consequence of the Bayes rule (
125
);
and relation (iv) results from (
39e
).

Step 2: controlling the integral in the decomposition (
127
).

In order to further control the right-hand side of expression (
127
), we need to evaluate the integral in (
127
).
To this end, we make a few observations.

•

To begin with, Lemma
1
tells us that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\big\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\big\|_{2}>5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\,|\,X_{t}=x\Big)\leq\exp\big(-c_{5}^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\big)

(128a)

for any quantity
c_{5}\geq 2
, provided that
c_{6}\geq 2c_{R}+c_{0}
.

•

A little algebra based on this relation allows one to bound
u
(cf. (
123
)) as follows:

\displaystyle\|u\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\mathbb{E}\left[\big\|\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}-x\big\|_{2}\,\big|\,X_{t}=x\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)+\frac{6(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T},

(128b)

where the last inequality arises from Lemma
1
.

Next, let us define

\mathcal{E}_{c}^{\mathsf{typical}}\coloneqq\Big\{x_{0}:\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}\leq 5c\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\Big\}

(129)

for any quantity
c>0
.
Then for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{c}^{\mathsf{typical}}
, it is clearly seen from (
128
) and (
39
) that

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{25c^{2}}{2}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\leq\frac{100c_{1}c^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{2}T}{T};

(130a)

\displaystyle\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{4(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)^{2}+\frac{36(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T

(130b)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c_{1}^{2}\log^{2}T}{T^{2}}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)^{2}+\frac{2304c_{1}^{2}}{T^{2}}\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{3}T,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|u\|_{2}\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5c(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\frac{30c(1-\alpha_{t})\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

(130c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20cc_{1}}{T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log^{3}T}+\frac{240cc_{1}\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{2}T}{T}.

(130d)

As a consequence, for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{c}^{\mathsf{typical}}
for
c\geq 2
, we have seen from (
130d
) and (
39
) that

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\leq\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{5c(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}+\frac{30c(1-\alpha_{t})\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

(131)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{20cc_{1}}{T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{3}T}+\frac{240cc_{1}}{T}\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{2}T\leq c\theta_{t}(x)d,

(132)

provided that

\frac{40c_{1}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\log^{\frac{3}{2}}T}{T}\leq\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d}\qquad\text{and}\qquad T\geq 480c_{1}\log^{2}T.

Step 2(a): proof of relation (
57
).

Substituting (
131
) into (
127
) and making use of (
39
) under our assumption on
T
yield

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{5c(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}+\frac{30c(1-\alpha_{t})}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\bigg)\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigg(\frac{5c(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}+\frac{30c(1-\alpha_{t})}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\bigg),

thus establishing (
57
) by taking
c=2
.

Step 2(b): proof of relation (
58a
).

Suppose now that

C_{10}\frac{\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log^{3}T}}{T}\leq 1

(133)

holds for some large enough constant
C_{10}>0
.
Under this additional condition, it can be easily verified that

\displaystyle\left|-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c_{10}\left(\theta_{t}(x)d\log T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}\right)\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

(134)

for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\mathsf{typical}}
(with
c=2
), where
c_{10}>0
is some sufficiently small constant.
Therefore,
the Taylor expansion
e^{-z}=1-z+O(z^{2})
(for all
|z|<1
) gives

\displaystyle\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}+O\bigg(\big(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\big)\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\bigg)

(135)

for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\mathsf{typical}}
,
which invokes (
134
) and (
130b
) (under the assumption (
133
)).
Combine (
135
) and (
132
) to show that

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}}+\int_{x_{0}\notin\mathcal{E}_{2}}\right)p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}-\frac{\|u\|_{2}^{2}-2u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\bigg(1-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{u^{\top}\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\big(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\big)\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\bigg)+O\left(\sum_{c=3}^{\infty}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{c}\backslash\mathcal{E}_{c-1}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\exp\left(c\theta_{t}(x)d\right)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big(\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}-\big\|\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\bigg)+O\Big(\exp\big(-\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big(\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}-\big\|\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\bigg),

(136)

where the penultimate relation holds since, according to (
128a
),

\displaystyle\sum_{c=3}^{\infty}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{c}\backslash\mathcal{E}_{c-1}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\exp\left(c\theta_{t}(x)d\right)\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{c=3}^{\infty}\exp\left(-c^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\right)\exp\left(c\theta_{t}(x)d\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{c=3}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}c^{2}\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\right)\leq\exp\big(-\theta_{t}(x)d\log T\big),

and the last line in (
136
) again utilizes (
39
) and the fact that
\theta_{t}(x)\geq c_{6}
for some large enough constant
c_{6}>0
.

Putting (
136
) and (
127
) together yields

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(x)}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+O\bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\bigg)-

\displaystyle\quad\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big(\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}-\big\|\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

as claimed.

B.1.2
Proof of relation (
58b
)

Consider any random vector
Y
.
To understand the density ratio
p_{\phi_{t}(Y)}(\phi_{t}(x))/p_{Y}(x)
, we make note of the transformation

\displaystyle p_{\phi_{t}(Y)}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{det}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\Big)^{-1}p_{Y}(x),

(137a)

\displaystyle p_{\phi_{t}^{\star}(Y)}\big(\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{det}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\Big)^{-1}p_{Y}(x),

(137b)

where
\frac{\partial\phi_{t}(x)}{\partial x}
and
\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}
denote the Jacobian matrices.
It thus suffices to control the quantity
\mathsf{det}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\Big)^{-1}
.

To begin with, recall from (
51
) and (
36
) that

\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)=x-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}g_{t}(x).

As a result, one can use (
37
) and (
38
) to derive

\displaystyle I-\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}J_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\Bigg\{I+\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\mid X_{t}=x\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\mid X_{t}=x\big]\Big)^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big)\big(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big)^{\top}\mid X_{t}=x\Big]\bigg\}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\bigg\{I+\frac{1}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}B\bigg\}.

(138)

This allows one to show that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tr}\Big(I-\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\Big)=\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\big(\big\|\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big)}{2(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}.

(139a)

Moreover, the matrix
B
defined in (
138
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|B\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big)\big(X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big)^{\top}\mid X_{t}=x\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}

due to Jensen’s inequality.
Taking this together with (
138
) and Lemma
1
reveals that

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}-I\Big\|\leq\Big\|\frac{\partial\phi^{\star}_{t}(x)}{\partial x}-I\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg(\sqrt{d}+\frac{\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\theta_{t}(x)d(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}{1-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}.

(139b)

Additionally, the Taylor expansion guarantees that for any
A
and
\Delta
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{det}\big(I+A+\Delta\big)

\displaystyle=1+\mathsf{Tr}(A)+O\big((\mathsf{Tr}(A))^{2}+\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+d^{3}\|A\|^{3}+d\|\Delta\|\big)

(140a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{det}\big(I+A+\Delta\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=1-\mathsf{Tr}(A)+O\big((\mathsf{Tr}(A))^{2}+\|A\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+d^{3}\|A\|^{3}+d\|\Delta\|\big)

(140b)

hold as long as
d\|A\|+d\|\Delta\|\leq c_{11}
for some small enough constant
c_{11}>0
.
The above properties taken collectively with (
51
) and (
48
) allow us to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\phi_{t}(Y)}(\phi_{t}(x))}{p_{Y}(x)}=\mathsf{det}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}(x)}{\partial x}\Big)^{-1}=\left(\mathsf{det}\bigg(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2}\Big[J_{s_{t}}(x)-J_{s_{t}^{\star}}(x)\Big]\bigg)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\left(\mathsf{det}\bigg(I+\frac{\partial\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}-I+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2}\Big[J_{s_{t}}(x)-J_{s_{t}^{\star}}(x)\Big]\bigg)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad=1-\mathsf{Tr}\Big(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}^{\star}(x)}{\partial x}-I\Big)+O\bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\theta^{3}d^{6}\log^{3}T\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{3}+(1-\alpha_{t})d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(x)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=1+\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\Big(\big\|\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big(x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big)\mathrm{d}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int_{x_{0}}p_{X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}}(x_{0}\,|\,x)\big\|x-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}x_{0}\Big)}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+O\bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\theta^{3}d^{6}\log^{3}T\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{3}+(1-\alpha_{t})d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(x)\bigg),

(141)

with the proviso that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}(1-\alpha_{t})\log T}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8c_{1}d^{2}\log^{2}T}{T}\leq c_{12}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(1-\alpha_{t})d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(x)\leq\frac{c_{1}d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(x)\log T}{T}\leq c_{12}

for some sufficiently small constant
c_{12}>0
(see (
39
)).

B.2
Proof of Lemma
5

In view of the definition (
67
),
one has

\displaystyle S_{k}(y_{T})\leq c_{14},\qquad\text{for any }k<\tau(y_{T}).

(142)

Suppose instead that (
69
) does not hold true, namely,

-\log q_{k}(y_{k})>2c_{6}d\log T
for some
k<\tau(y_{T})
, and we would like to show that this leads to contradiction.

Towards this, let
1<t\leq k
be the smallest time step obeying

\displaystyle\theta_{t}(y_{t})=\max\bigg\{-\frac{\log q_{t}(y_{t})}{d\log T},c_{6}\bigg\}>2c_{6}=2\theta_{1}(y_{1}),

(143)

where the last identity holds since
-\log q_{1}(y_{1})\leq c_{6}d\log T
and hence
\theta_{1}(y_{1})=\max\big\{-\frac{\log q_{1}(y_{1})}{d\log T},c_{6}\big\}=c_{6}
.
We claim that
t
necessarily obeys

\displaystyle 2c_{6}<\theta_{t}(y_{t})\leq 4c_{6}.

(144)

Assuming the validity of Claim (
144
) for the moment,
it necessarily satisfies

\theta_{1}(y_{1}),\theta_{2}(y_{2}),\cdots,\theta_{t}(y_{t})\in[c_{6},4c_{6}].

According to the relations (
59
) and (
142
), we derive

\displaystyle c_{6}=\theta_{1}(y_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\theta_{t}(y_{t})-\theta_{1}(y_{1})=-\frac{\log q_{t}(y_{t})}{d\log T}-\theta_{1}(y_{1})\leq\frac{-\log q_{t}(y_{t})+\log q_{1}(y_{1})}{d\log T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d\log T}\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\big(\log q_{j}(y_{j})-\log q_{j+1}(y_{j+1})\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}+C_{10}\left\{\frac{d\log^{3}T}{T}+\frac{S_{\tau(y_{T})-1}(y_{T})}{d\log T}\right\}<3c_{1}

under our sample size condition. This, however, cannot possibly hold if
c_{6}\geq 3c_{1}
as assumed for Lemma
5
.

To finish up, it suffices to justify Claim (
144
).
In order to see this, suppose instead that
\theta_{t}(y_{t})>4c_{6}
.
Given relation (
142
) that
S_{k}(y_{T})\leq c_{14}
, it can be readily seen from
(
57
), (
142
) as well as the learning rate properties (
39
) that

\displaystyle\theta_{t-1}(y_{t-1})

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}(y_{t})+\theta_{t-1}(y_{t-1})-\theta_{t}(y_{t})

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}(y_{t})+\theta_{t-1}(y_{t-1})+\frac{\log p_{t}(y_{t})}{d\log T}\geq\theta_{t}(y_{t})-\frac{\log p_{t-1}(y_{t-1})-\log p_{t}(y_{t})}{d\log T}

\displaystyle\geq\theta_{t}(y_{t})-\frac{4c_{1}\Big(5\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{\theta_{t}(y_{t})d\log T}+60\theta_{t}(y_{t})d\log T\Big)}{dT}-\frac{\log 2}{d\log T}

\displaystyle\geq\theta_{t}(y_{t})-\frac{4c_{1}\Big(5\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{d\log T}+60d\log T\Big)}{dT}\theta_{t}(y_{t})-\frac{\log 2}{d\log T}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{2}\theta_{t}(y_{t})>2c_{6},

which is contradictory with the assumption that
t
is the smallest step obeying
\theta_{t}(y_{t})>2c_{6}
. Thus, we complete the proof of relation (
69
) as required.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
6

Next, consider any
y_{T}
, with
\{y_{T-1},\cdots,y_{1}\}
being the associated deterministic sequence (cf. (
53
))).
As an immediate consequence of Lemma
5
and the definition (
56
) of
\theta_{t}(\cdot)
, one has

\theta_{t}(y_{t})\leq 2c_{6},\qquad\forall t<\tau(y_{T})

(145)

We then intend to invoke Lemma
4
to control the term of interest.
To do so, note that Lemma
1
, (
39
) and the definition (
67
) of
\tau(y_{T})
taken together reveal that:
for all
t<\tau(y_{T})
one has

\frac{d(1-\alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\lesssim\frac{d\log T}{T}=o(1),

\displaystyle\theta_{t}(y_{t})^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{\theta_{t}(y_{t})d\log T}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)+\theta_{t}(y_{t})^{3}d^{6}\Big(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{3}\log^{3}T+(1-\alpha_{t})d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(y_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{d^{2}\log^{4}T}{T^{2}}+\frac{d^{6}\log^{6}T}{T^{3}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{d\log^{3}T}}{T}+\frac{d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(y_{t})\log T}{T}=o(1),

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\Big(\big\|\int\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\,|\,X_{t}=y_{t}\big]\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\,|\,X_{t}=y_{t}\big]\Big)}{(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\int\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|X_{t}-\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\,|\,X_{t}=y_{t}\big]}{(\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t})(1-\overline{\alpha}_{t})}\right|\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})d\log T}{\alpha_{t}-\overline{\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim\frac{d\log^{2}T}{T}=o(1).

With these bounds in mind, applying relations (
58a
) and (
58b
) in Lemma
4
leads to

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}Y_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(y_{t})\big)}{p_{Y_{t}}(y_{t})}\bigg(\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(y_{t})\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(y_{t})}\bigg)^{-1}=\frac{p_{\phi_{t}(Y_{t})}\big(\phi_{t}(y_{t})\big)}{p_{Y_{t}}(y_{t})}\bigg(\frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t-1}}\big(\phi_{t}(y_{t})\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(y_{t})}\bigg)^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad=1+O\Bigg(\frac{d^{2}\log^{4}T}{T^{2}}+\frac{d^{6}\log^{6}T}{T^{3}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{d\log^{3}T}}{T}+\frac{d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(y_{t})\log T}{T}\Bigg)

for all
t<\tau(y_{T})
.
Using the fact that
y_{t-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\phi_{t}(y_{t})
and invoking the relation (
55
),
we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}{q_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+O\Bigg(\frac{d^{2}\log^{4}T}{T^{2}}+\frac{d^{6}\log^{6}T}{T^{3}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(y_{t})\sqrt{d\log^{3}T}}{T}+\frac{d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(y_{t})\log T}{T}\Bigg)\right\}\frac{p_{t}(y_{t})}{q_{t}(y_{t})}

for any
t<\tau(y_{T})
.
By abbreviating
\tau=\tau(y_{T})
for notational simplicity,
we reach

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1}(y_{1})}{p_{1}(y_{1})}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+O\Bigg(\frac{d^{2}\log^{4}T}{T}+\frac{d^{6}\log^{6}T}{T^{2}}+S_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})\Bigg)\right\}\frac{q_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})}{p_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})}

\displaystyle\in\left[\frac{p_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})}{2q_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})},\frac{2p_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})}{q_{\tau-1}(y_{\tau-1})}\right],

(146a)

and similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{q_{k}(y_{k})}{2p_{k}(y_{k})}\leq\frac{q_{1}(y_{1})}{p_{1}(y_{1})}\leq 2\frac{q_{k}(y_{k})}{p_{k}(y_{k})},\qquad\forall k<\tau.

(146b)

B.4
Proof of Lemma
7

In the following, we shall tackle
\mathcal{I}_{2}
,
\mathcal{I}_{3}
and
\mathcal{I}_{4}
separately.
Throughout this proof, we shall abbreviate
\tau=\tau(Y_{T})
(cf. (
67
)) whenever it is clear from the context.

The sub-collection in
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

By virtue of the definition (
72a
) of
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\right\}\bigg]\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\right\}\frac{S_{\tau}(Y_{T})}{c_{14}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{=}\frac{\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{\tau}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{\tau}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{2},\tau\geq t}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{2}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim p_{t}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\lesssim}\big(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi}}+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score}}\big)\log T.

(147)

Here, (i) follows since
S_{\tau}\big(y_{T}\big)\geq c_{14}
in
\mathcal{I}_{2}
(see (
72a
));
(ii) comes from the definition of
S_{t}(\cdot)
(see (
54
));
(iii) holds since (by repeating the same proof arguments as for (
70
) as long as
2c_{14}
is small enough)

\frac{p_{1}(y_{1})}{q_{1}(y_{1})}\leq\frac{2p_{t}(y_{t})}{q_{t}(y_{t})},\qquad\forall t\leq\tau;

and (iv) arises from (
49
).

The sub-collection in
\mathcal{I}_{3}
.

With regards to
\mathcal{I}_{3}
(cf. (
72b
)), we can derive the following bound in a way similar to (
147
):

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\bigg]\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\frac{\xi_{\tau}(Y_{T})}{c_{14}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\log T}{c_{14}T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},\tau}(Y_{\tau})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},\tau}(Y_{\tau})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{\tau-1}(Y_{\tau-1})}{p_{\tau-1}(Y_{\tau-1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},\tau}(Y_{\tau})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},\tau}(Y_{\tau})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}{p_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\ind\{\tau=t\}\bigg]

(148)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\frac{16\log T}{c_{14}T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{\tau}(Y_{\tau})}{p_{\tau}(Y_{\tau})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{3}\right\}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},\tau}(Y_{\tau})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},\tau}(Y_{\tau})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{16\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim p_{t}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{16\log T}{c_{14}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim q_{t}}\Big[d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi},t}(Y_{t})+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score},t}(Y_{t})\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(d\varepsilon_{\mathsf{Jacobi}}+\sqrt{d\log T}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{score}}\right)\log T.

(149)

Here, (i) comes from (
72b
),
(ii) arises from (
70b
),
whereas (iii) is a consequence of (
72b
).

The sub-collection in
\mathcal{I}_{4}
.

We now turn attention to
\mathcal{I}_{4}
(cf. (
72c
)), towards which we find it helpful to define

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\{y_{T}:\xi_{t}\big(y_{T}\big)<c_{14}\Big\}

(150a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\{y_{T}:\xi_{t}\big(y_{T}\big)\geq c_{14},\frac{q_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}{p_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}\leq\frac{8q_{t}(y_{t})}{p_{t}(y_{t})}\bigg\}

(150b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\{y_{T}:\xi_{t}\big(y_{T}\big)\geq c_{14},\frac{q_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}{p_{t-1}(y_{t-1})}>\frac{8q_{t}(y_{t})}{p_{t}(y_{t})}\bigg\}

(150c)

for each
2\leq t\leq T
.
Equipped with the above definitions, we first make the observation that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{4}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{\tau-1}(Y_{1})}{p_{\tau-1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{4}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}{p_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{I}_{4}\right\}\ind\{\tau=t\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}{p_{t-1}(Y_{t-1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}\bigg],

(151)

where the first inequality follows from (
70b
),
and the last line comes from the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{4}
(cf. (
72c
)) and
\mathcal{J}_{3,t}
(cf. (
150c
)).
For notational simplicity, let us define, for
2\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle h_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}.

In view of the second inequality in (
150c
), one has

h_{t-1}>8h_{t}
as long as
y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}

\displaystyle<\sum_{t=2}^{T}h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}+\frac{1}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}-\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}h_{t}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\bigg(\Big(h_{t-1}-h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\right\}-h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\right\}\Big)-\Big(h_{t}-h_{t}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\right\}-h_{t}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\right\}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\big(h_{t}-{h}_{t-1}\big)\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\cup\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\right\}+\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\big(h_{t-1}-h_{t}\big).

Here, the second line holds true since, for all
t
, one has (i)
\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\cup\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\cup\mathcal{J}_{3,t}=\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and (ii)
\mathcal{J}_{1,t}
,

\mathcal{J}_{2,t}
and
\mathcal{J}_{3,t}
are disjoint.
Substituting this into (
151
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{1}(Y_{1})}{p_{1}(Y_{1})}\ind\left\{Y_{1}\in\mathcal{E},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}\bigg]\leq 2\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{3,t}\right\}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\Big(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\cup\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\right\}\big]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t-1}\ind\left\{Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\cup\mathcal{J}_{2,t}\right\}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{8}{7}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\Big(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t-1}\big]-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t}\big]\Big).

(152)

In order to further bound (
152
),
we make note of a few basic facts. Firstly, the identity below holds:

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\bigg]=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim p_{t}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\bigg]=1,\qquad 2\leq t\leq T.

Secondly, by defining the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\{y:q_{t}(y)>\exp\big(-c_{6}d\log T\big)\Big\},\qquad 2\leq t\leq T,

(153)

we can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\big[h_{t}\ind\left\{Y_{t}\notin\mathcal{E}_{t},Y_{T}\in\mathcal{J}_{1,t}\right\}\big]\leq\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{T}\sim p_{T}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\ind\left\{Y_{t}\notin\mathcal{E}_{t}\right\}\bigg]=\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{t}\sim p_{t}}\bigg[\frac{q_{t}(Y_{t})}{p_{t}(Y_{t})}\ind\left\{Y_{t}\notin\mathcal{E}_{t}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathbb{P}_{Y_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left\{Y_{t}\notin\mathcal{E}_{t}\right\}=\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathbb{P}_{X_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left\{X_{t}\notin\mathcal{E}_{t}\right\}
